## Document 004: 10月企業物価指数 ～前年比＋7.1％、電力や石油・石炭製品を中心に幾分加速～

Source PDF: all report pdf/10月企業物価指数 ～前年比＋7.1％、電力や石油・石炭製品を中心に幾分加速～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-11-12
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Economic Indicators

2026 年 11 月 12 日

1/2 
10 月企業物価指数 
～前年比＋7.1％、電力や石油・石炭製品を中心に幾分加速～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  阿原 健一郎 
（℡：050-5470-4868）

1. 国内企業物価指数は幾分加速 
 本日公表された 10 月の国内企業物価指数は前年比＋7.1％と、前月（同＋6.8％）から 
伸びが幾分加速した。前年比寄与度で内訳を見ると、玄米や豚肉などの「農林水産物」のマイナ
ス寄与が前月から拡大した一方、「電力・都市ガス・水道」や「石油・石炭製品」、「化学製品」の
プラス寄与が前月から拡大し、ヘッドラインを押し上げた（図表１）。 
 背景には、原油やナフサの価格上昇が石油製品や化学製品へ波及していることに加え、電力に
おいても、過去の輸入燃料価格の上昇がタイムラグを伴って燃料費調整単価に反映され、全体を
押し上げていることがある。農林水産物の価格下落が一部で下押し要因となっているものの、エ
ネルギー関連を中心としたコスト上昇圧力は依然として根強い。

２. 輸入物価指数はわずかに加速 
 10 月の輸入物価指数は前年比＋24.8％と、前月（同＋23.8％）から伸びがわずかに加速した。
「石油・石炭・天然ガス」のプラス寄与が前月から拡大し、ヘッドラインを押し上げた（図表
２）。 
 為替変動の影響を除いた契約通貨ベースでも同＋19.2％と、前月（同＋17.3％）から伸びが拡大
しており、海外の資源価格上昇が輸入物価を押し上げている。輸入コストの増加が時間差を伴っ
て国内の製品価格に波及することを踏まえると、先行きも価格転嫁を通じて消費者物価への上昇
圧力が残る可能性がある。

以 上

## Document 005: 10月宏观数据点评：内外需延续分化格局

Source PDF: all report pdf/10月宏观数据点评：内外需延续分化格局.pdf
Report source: 银泰证券
Date: 2026-11-18
Pages: 11
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

请务必阅读正文之后的免责条款部分 研究专业创新 如意投资随心
宏观研究
宏观点评
内外需延续分化格局
——10月宏观数据点评
事件：2026 年 11 月 16日，国家统计局发布 10 月份国内宏观经济数据。10
月份，全国规模以上工业增加值同比增长 4.8%，社会消费品零售总额同比增
长 0.4%；1—10 月全国固定资产投资（不含农户）同比下降 8.1%，房地产
开发投资同比下降 21.6%。
核心观点：
 10 月国内经济内外需分化格局延续，“供强需弱”特征进一步显现。各
口径表现情况看，出口高增与新兴产业快速发展继续对工业生产形成有
力支撑，10 月规模以上工业增加值同比增长 4.8%，高技术制造业、装备
制造业增加值分别增长 17.6%和 10.6%；但内需端进一步走弱，社零受
假期错位影响增速再度放缓，10 月同比增长 0.4%、较上月回落 0.7 个百
分点，1—10 月固定资产投资同比下降 8.1%，房地产开发投资下降
21.6%，降幅较上月分别扩大 0.9和 1.0个百分点。

 往后看，伴随各项稳增长政策落地，包括新型政策性金融工具、专项债
等资金加快形成实物工作量，以及房地产因城施策与城市更新持续推进
等，后期国内经济增长动能有望边际改善。但政策力度与节奏、外需持
续性及海外货币政策变化仍是主要不确定性来源。
 风险提示：1、政策进度不及预期；2、出口回落超预期；3、美联储政策
紧缩超预期。
2026 年 11 月 18 日

1、外需与新动能继续对工业生产形成支撑
10 月份，全国规模以上工业增加值同比增长
4.8%，增速较上月的 5.5%回落 0.7 个百分点，低于
市场一致预期的增长5.3%，1—10月累计增长5.3%。
10 月外需与新动能继续对工业生产形成支撑。
以美元计当月国内出口同比增长35.2%，受此带动规
模以上工业企业出口交货值同比名义增长18.6%，外
需对工业生产的拉动作用持续显现。另一方面，10
月份高技术制造业增加值同比增长17.6%，装备制造
业增长 10.6%，分别快于全部规模以上工业 12.8 和
5.8个百分点；3D打印设备、锂离子电池、工业机器
人产量同比分别增长85.9%、30.0%和26.0%，新能源
汽车产量增长17.1%，新动能产业继续为工业生产注
入强劲动能。
图表 1 规模以上工业增加值增速（%） 图表 2 规上工业企业出口交货值增速（%）
数据来源：同花顺 iFinD、银泰证券研究所

分三大门类看，10 月采矿业增加值同比增长
0.7%，制造业增长 5.4%，电力、热力、燃气及水生
产和供应业增长4.4%，较上月分别放缓0.6、0.6和
1.2个百分点。
分行业看，10 月份 41 个大类行业中有 28 个行
业增加值保持同比增长，增长面为68.3%。其中装备
与电子相关行业景气度整体领先，计算机、通信和其
他电子设备制造业增长19.4%，专用设备制造业增长
11.5%，铁路、船舶、航空航天和其他运输设备制造
业增长9.2%，通用设备制造业增长8.5%，电气机械
和器材制造业增长8.1%，汽车制造业增长 0.9%。传
统周期行业多数继续承压，有色金属冶炼和压延加工
业下降4.4%，煤炭开采和洗选业下降2.9%，非金属
矿物制品业下降 2.3%，黑色金属冶炼和压延加工业
下降1.7%，化学原料和化学制品制造业仅增长1.3%。

2、假期错位影响社零增速再度放缓
10月份，社会消费品零售总额同比增长0.4%，
增速较上月的1.1%回落0.7个百分点，低于市场一
致预期的0.8%，1—10月累计增长1.0%。当月社零
增速放缓，中秋假期错位带来明显扰动，但国内消费
需求偏弱同样是重要原因。
按消费类型分，10月商品零售额同比增长0.3%
（上月为+1.0%），餐饮增长0.7%（上月为+2.1%），
增速分别放缓0.7 和1.4 个百分点。10月份限额以
上单位消费品零售额同比下降4.9%，降幅较上月扩
大2.8个百分点，自4 月以来持续处于负增长区间。
主要商品类别中，汽车类同比下降21.4%（上月为
-21.2%），继续对社零整体形成拖累；此外通讯器材
类同比增长6.5%（上月为31.1%），增速较上月明显
放缓，家用电器和音像器材类增长2.5%（上月为
1.9%）边际有所改善。其它商品类别中，烟酒类增长
2.5%（上月为13.8%），饮料类增长0.3%（上月为

5.0%），文化办公用品类增长4.1%（上月为7.9%），
中西药品类增长3.7%（上月为5.0%），化妆品类增
长2.8%（上月为5.4%），增速较上月均有不同程度
的放缓。

3、固投延续调整，新动能投资较快增长
1—10月份，全国固定资产投资（不含农户）同
比下降 8.1%，降幅较 1—9 月的-7.2%扩大 0.9个百分

### Page 7

请务必阅读正文之后的免责条款部分 研究专业创新 如意投资随心
点，低于市场一致预期的-6.8。民间固定资产投资同
比下降 11.4%，降幅继续扩大。
分领域看，基础设施投资同比下降 5.1%，降幅
较 1—9月扩大 0.9个百分点；制造业投资下降 2.9%，
降幅扩大 0.8个百分点。
结构上看，投资向新动能集中的特征进一步强
化。1—10 月知识产权产品投资同比增长 9.2%，占
全部投资比重 16.2%，拉动全部投资增长 1.3个百分
点；设备工器具购置投资增长 8.9%，占比 20.3%、
比上年同期提高 3.2个百分点，拉动全部投资增长 1.5
个百分点。高技术产业投资同比增长 4.0%，其中信
息服务业投资增长 20.5%、航空、航天器及设备制造
业增长 10.2%、电子及通信设备制造业增长 6.6%。
基建投资落地进程偏慢，1—10月电力、热力、
燃气及水生产和供应业投资下降 7.7%（1—9 月为
-6.8%），交通运输、仓储和邮政业增长 0.7%（1—9
月为+1.9%），水利、环境和公共设施管理业下降

14.4%（1—9月为-12.8%），投资增速较上月均有不
同程度的放缓。

4、房地产延续筑底，开发投资降幅扩大
1—10 月份，全国房地产开发投资同比下降
21.6%，降幅较1—9 月的-20.6%扩大1.0个百分点。
房屋施工面积下降13.0%，新开工面积下降24.4%，
竣工面积下降 26.6%，开发端各环节仍全面处于低

位。
销售端看，1—10 月新建商品房销售面积同比
下降13.7%，降幅较1—9月扩大 0.5个百分点；销
售额下降14.3%，降幅扩大0.4个百分点。
相对积极的是，二手房交易持续改善。1—10
月全国二手房交易网签面积 67971 万平方米、同比
增长11.4%，增速较1—9月加快 0.7个百分点。
房价方面，10月份一线城市新建商品住宅销售
价格环比由上月上涨0.1%转为持平，其中上海、深
圳分别上涨0.2%和0.1%，广州持平、北京下降0.1%；
二线城市环比由持平转为下降0.1%，三线城市下降
0.2%、降幅与上月相同；70个大中城市中新建商品
住宅价格环比上涨城市14个，比上月减少3 个。
二手住宅方面，一线城市环比上涨0.2%、涨幅
与上月持平（上海+0.5%、广州+0.2%、深圳+0.1%、
北京持平），二线、三线城市均下降0.3%；二手住
宅价格环比上涨城市7 个，比上月减少2 个。
同比看，一二三线城市新建商品住宅价格降幅

分别收窄0.4、0.4和0.3个百分点，二手住宅价格
降幅分别收窄1.1、0.4和0.4个百分点，一线城市
价格企稳趋势继续得到确认，但全国层面地产仍处
于筑底阶段。

## Document 016: 2.3万亿美元“灰飞烟灭”！美国芯片股上一次跌这么惨，还是2001年，英伟达已经“跌掉一个亚马逊”

Source PDF: all report pdf/2.3万亿美元“灰飞烟灭”！美国芯片股上一次跌这么惨，还是2001年，英伟达已经“跌掉一个亚马逊”.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-21
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

本周，费城半导体指数跌逾 10% ，芯⽚板块市值蒸发约 2.3 万亿美元，创 2001 年以来最惨
单周表现。英伟达股价连续七周下跌，⾃ 10 ⽉⾼点以来市值缩⽔约 2.7 万亿美元。英伟达收
紧融资⽀持、 Anthropic IPO 抽⾛流动性，美联储鹰派⽴场再添压⼒， AI 交易遭遇集中抛
售，市场对增⻓前景的担忧升温。
摘要：
AI 交易的三⼤⽀柱同时松动，美国科技股遭遇猛烈抛售，市场对 AI 泡沫和经济增⻓放缓的担忧迅
速升温。
本周，费城半导体指数（ SOX ）重挫逾 10% ，创 2001 年以来最惨烈的单周表现，芯⽚板块整体
市值蒸发约 2.3 万亿美元。英伟达股价连续第七周下跌，创 2008 年以来最⻓连跌纪录，⾃历史⾼
点以来市值缩⽔约 2.7 万亿美元，相当于跌掉⼀个亚⻢逊。
抛售潮迅速蔓延⾄整个美股市场。标普 500 指数连续七个交易⽇下跌，单周跌幅超过 9% ，创近
年来最⼤单周跌幅；纳斯达克 100 指数单周下跌逾 10% ，年内涨幅悉数回吐。 VIX ⼀度突破 40 ，

市场恐慌情绪急剧升温。
值得注意的是，英伟达财报表现亮眼、 Anthropic 创纪录 IPO 相继登场，却都未能提振市场信
⼼。 AI 巨头融资态度转变、 IPO 抽⾛市场流动性，加上美联储鹰派⽴场不变，令市场开始重新审
视这轮 AI ⾏情的⽀撑基础。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

芯⽚股遭遇 2001 年以来最惨单周抛售，英伟达跌掉⼀个亚⻢逊
本周，费城半导体指数下跌逾 10% ，芯⽚板块市值蒸发约 2.3 万亿美元，创 2001 年以来最惨烈的
单周表现。
英伟达同样未能幸免。尽管公司最新季度业绩和业绩指引均超出市场预期，股价仍连续七周下
跌，创 2008 年以来最⻓连跌纪录。周五股价进⼀步⼤跌，收于 2025 年 5 ⽉以来最低⽔平。
从 10 ⽉历史⾼点以来，英伟达股价累计下跌约 47% ，市值缩⽔约 2.7 万亿美元，相当于⼀个亚⻢
逊。⽬前公司市值约 3.03 万亿美元，已低于 Alphabet 。
值得注意的是，英伟达基本⾯并未明显转弱：公司连续第 17 个季度超越卖⽅预期，第四季度营
收指引达到 1300 亿美元，⾼于市场预期的 1250 亿美元，还将 2028 财年增⻓预期从 70% 上调⾄
75% 。市场担忧的焦点，正在转向 AI ⽣态系统的融资和增⻓模式。
英伟达收缩 “AI 央⾏ ” ⻆⾊，⽣态系统融资⽀持转向审慎
据 MarketWatch 报道，英伟达新任董事⻓ Harvey Jones 明确表⽰，⻩仁勋不会回归公司，同时

释放出收紧客⼾融资⽀持的信号：只有在项⽬具备⼟地、电⼒和建筑等实体条件后，公司才会
提供担保⽀持。
⾼盛 TMT 团队将此解读为英伟达正在主动收缩 “AI 央⾏ ” ⻆⾊。此前，英伟达不仅是芯⽚供应商，
也通过融资担保等⽅式⽀持 AI ⽣态系统。如今态度转向审慎，可能冲击依赖英伟达担保或承购⽀
持的纯新云计算公司。

### Page 3

Citadel Securities 的 Nohshad Shah 指出，像⻩仁勋这样的创始⼈能够推动公司承担职业经理⼈
难以向董事会交代的⾼⻛险投资。⾼盛 Rich Privorotsky 则认为，英伟达此前被视为 AI ⽣态系
统的 “ 多头看跌期权 ” ，如今这⼀⻆⾊已经逆转，甚⾄成为市场的 “ 空头看跌期权 ” 。
Anthropic 创纪录 IPO 反成流动性⿊洞， AI 资产遭遇集中抛售
Anthropic 原本被视为 AI 产业信⼼的⼜⼀次验证。这家 Claude 开发商以⾼于发⾏区间的价格定
价，融资规模达 1073 亿美元（含向芯⽚和云供应商的 260 亿美元配售），完全稀释估值约 1.5 万亿
美元。
但 IPO 未能提振市场，反⽽引发流动性冲击。 Anthropic 股价以 111.50 美元开盘，随后迅速遭到抛
售。纳斯达克 100 指数从盘前上涨 2% 转为下跌 2% ， AI 股票篮⼦也从上涨 4% 转为下跌 2% ，标普
500 指数当⽇市值蒸发约 1.3 万亿美元。

市场⼈⼠认为，投资者为参与 IPO 提前筹集资⾦，加剧了其他资产的抛售。⻩⾦、加密货币、铜
和英伟达股票均在 IPO 前数⽇遭到卖出。彭博援引分析指出，⽐特币在发⾏前的异常下跌，与投
资者筹集认购资⾦的⾏为相符。
⾄周五收盘， Anthropic 股价跌⾄ 83.10 美元附近，⼏乎回到 83 美元的发⾏价，较周四 115.10 美元
的⾼点下跌逾 25% 。承销商已全额⾏使超额配售权，额外出售 1.47 亿股，意味着稳定股价的潜
在⼯具进⼀步减少。

### Page 4

美联储鹰派施压，市场脆弱结构放⼤跌势
美联储本周进⼀步强化紧缩信号。理事 Waller 重申⽀持加息 50 个基点的⽴场，指出约 70% 的核⼼
服务项⽬涨幅仍超过 3% ，美联储可能需要收回去年全部三次预防性降息，甚⾄采取更多⾏动。
他还表⽰，可能需要通过政策⾏动 “ 震慑市场 ” 。
10 ⽉会议纪要也显⽰，部分官员曾倾向于加息，许多与会者认为 AI 需求持续对供给形成压⼒并
推⾼价格。彭博数据显⽰，美联储官员鹰鸽表态指标已升⾄ 2023 年以来最鹰派⽔平。
市场结构则进⼀步放⼤了抛售。据⾼盛 Prime 数据，基本⾯多空净杠杆本⽉再下降 7.6 个百分
点，降⾄五年多来最低⽔平。做市商在周五期权到期前持有⼤量空头伽⻢头⼨，使下跌更容易
触发进⼀步对冲卖盘。
⾼盛数据显⽰，周三标普 500 期货盘⼝深度平均仅为 420 万美元，是过去四年来流动性最差的交
易⽇之⼀。不过，资⾦并未全⾯撤离：截⾄ 11 ⽉ 18 ⽇当周，全球股票基⾦净流⼊ 592 亿美元，科
技基⾦净流⼊ 123 亿美元，显⽰部分投资者仍在逢低买⼊。

债市转向增⻓担忧， AI 交易⾯临重新定价
与股市同步下跌的还有美债收益率。尽管美联储官员持续释放鹰派信号， 10 年期美债收益率仍
连续五个交易⽇下⾏，从 5.17% 降⾄ 4.90% ，结束了⾃ 2007 年以来最⻓的持续⾼于 5% 的时期。
⾼盛 Rich Privorotsky 认为，⻓端收益率可能已经开始反映政策失误的⻛险。收益率下⾏的同
时，盈亏平衡通胀率也在收缩，这⼀组合表明市场关注点正从通胀转向经济增⻓和流动性。
换⾔之，市场担忧正在发⽣变化：投资者不再只关注通胀顽固和⾼利率，也开始担⼼紧缩政策
可能过度，以及 AI 投资热潮能否持续⽀撑经济增⻓。
Baird 董事总经理 Ted Mortonson 表⽰， 2026 年上半年的市场充斥着过度亢奋、投机和⾃满，⽽
本⽉以来市场情绪已经转向恐惧与恐慌。
Privorotsky 的判断则更为谨慎：在央⾏政策真正转向之前，市场反弹仍可能是卖出机会。

### Page 5

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

Realtek Semiconductor  | Asia Pacific
Asia Pacific Summit 2026
Feedback
Realtek remains relatively resilient despite sluggish PC demand, supported by its
diversified product portfolio and platform agnostic strategy. Near-term customer
inventory adjustments appear to reflect shipment timing rather than underlying
demand weakness. Meanwhile, elevated legacy DRAM prices continue to pressure
device BOM costs, potentially delaying broader adoption of edge Al and other

consumer applications
Looking ahead, edge server and edge Al represent structural growth opportunities,
supported by ongoing development in high-speed SerDes and integrated Al
solutions. Realtek also sees opportunities to strengthen its position in higher-end TV
SoCs amid competition. A normalization in memory prices could provide an
additional catalyst for downstream demand recovery.

## Document 172: 261123_ubs_EMC

### Page 1

ab 23 November 2026
Global Research
Initiation of Coverage
Elite Material
Manifesting AI-driven upcycle; initiate at Buy
Initiate on TW CCL - EMC: major beneficiary of  structural CCL material upgrade
We initiate EMC with a Buy rating and PT NT$5,000. The company is a global CCL leader
that enables AI systems with high-speed and low-loss materials. We see booming GPU/
ASIC demand driving not just CCL consumption but also a shift toward higher-grade

CCL products to meet high-speed and signal integrity requirements. M8 CCL has
become standard for Blackwell and will migrate to M9/M10 for Feynman.  We anticipate
these structural upgrades and capacity expansion to support  62% sales CAGR   through
'26-'28E; and anticipate product mix shift toward  M7+ to drive margin expansion.
Resilient fundamentals underpinned by strong positioning
Our industry studies suggest EMC is well positioned across AI GPU/ASIC projects. It was

the dominant supplier for Nvidia Hopper OAM/UBB boards. Doosan has begun to supply
compute tray from Blackwell, while EMC and SYTECH supplies the switch tray. We
believe EMC is also one of the major suppliers for Google TPU (splitting with Panasonic)
and AWS Trainium (other suppliers including a TW CCL supplier with potential new
entrants such as SYTECH and Nan Ya Plastics). We see AI platforms ramping up
production in H226 that are enlarging the supply/demand imbalance, driving further

CCL pricing uplifts. We believe EMC's capacity scale and major projects' share allocation
backed by its upstream sourcing will support its growth realization of strong AI demand.
Investing in M8+ for future growth with margin expansion
As a major supplier for  GPU and ASIC platforms, EMC is expanding capacity to capture
future growth. The company announced an investment total of NT$98.6bn through
2027-2030F to add capacity across Taiwan, Mainland China, and Malaysia, where its

total capacity will increase from 5,850k sheets/month in 2026F to 15,250k sheets/
month by 2030F, and these additions would be supporting M8+ CCL demand. M7+
increased from 55–60% of product mix in Q126 to 65–70% in Q326. Looking ahead,
management guides for M7+ to reach 70–80%  in 2027F as AI server specs continue to
move up the material ladder. We expect earnings to grow at 82% CAGR through 2026-
2028E, outpacing topline growth of 62% for the same periods, supported by favourable

product mix shift. Meanwhile, EMC is attempting to build ABF substrate CCL capacity
and going through qualification in H226. We believe high-end CCL remains its key focus
and note that these capacity additions are fungible for M8/M9 high-speed CCL
production, and we have not yet factored in ABF substrate CCL business potential.
Valuation: Initiate at Buy with PT$5,000
In light of market concerns on AI slowdown, we note that our estimates are below

consensus factoring in slower capacity ramp (10-30% lower than announced plans).
Our NT$5,000 PT is based on 12x 2028E PE, which we think is conservative at the low-
end of its  historical trading range of 7-20x. Despite more conservative estimates, still see
attractive risk-reward back by  37%'26-'30E earnings CAGR, implying only 0.3x PEG.

### Page 2

Elite Material UBS Research
Thesis Map     UBS                    Research     THESIS MAP a guide to our thinking and what´s where in this report
Q: Is favourable pricing environment and current CCL industry upcycle sustainable?
We continue to see AI CCL content value increases as computing and networking/interconnect
demands surges. Extended equipment lead time and qualification process along with increasing
manufacturing challenges are also suggesting more constrained upstream inputs. We expect

persisting CCL industry shortage to drive better pricing power and believe high-end CCL have
entered a spec-driven structural upgrade cycle, not a temporary commodity uptick.
Q: Could high-end CCL drive the next phase of growth for Elite Material?
Yes. Among Taiwanese suppliers, we believe EMC is best positioned to capitalise on rapid AI
deployment based on its competitive high-end material offerings across GPU/ASIC platforms. We
expect EMC will continue to benefit from growing AI-driven computing/networking demand and

high-end CCL spec upgrades as a key supplier for major GPU and several US cloud service providers'
ASIC CCL materials. We are positive on its solid business fundamentals and see its business scale,
customer exposure, further pricing uplifts, favourable product mix shift could drive strong earnings
expansion in the next few years.
Among CCL supply chain, we believe EMC is best positioned for further re-rating given its leading
position in high-end CCL materials with capacity scale, high-end product offerings, and sourcing

capability. The company has board customer exposure among major GPU and ASIC platforms, best
positioned to capitalize AI-driven spec upgrades.
Top 6 CSP capex spending has revised up another leg, strengthening visibility into AI-related CCL
demand. Hyperscaler capex grew 25% QoQ/ 101% YoY in Q326 to US$252.7bn, with UBS 2026/27E
forecasts revised up 2%/18% to imply +96%/+74% YoY growth to US$897bn/US$1.6tn.
Meanwhile, the pricing backdrop remains supportive,   with Taiwanese CCL suppliers raising prices by

10-30% QoQ in Q126 and Q226, followed by further double-digit increases in August and
September. EMC’s latest commentary reinforces this trend,  noting 5-20% price increases across its
portfolio in Q426F and further upside possible should the supply-demand mismatch persist.
EMC’s share price has fallen 56% from its Aug peak (vs. TAIEX -21%), amid the broader tech sell-off
and rising concerns over slower AI spending. The stock now trades at 10x/7x our 2027/2028E P/E,

below its long-term historical avg. of 13.5x and the lower-end of the  AI cycle 10-48x, despite our
'27/'28 EPS estimates already  c.4%/4% below consensus. We believe current valuation increasingly
prices in slower AI demand, while giving insufficient credit to EMC’s technology leadership and
continued M8/M9/M10 upgrades, which we believe warrant a premium to CCL peers. Reflecting
recent market sentiment, we conservatively value EMC at  12x 2028E P/E, around its historical

median. This remains attractive given 37% 2026-30E earnings CAGR and implies  0.3x PEG.

Elite Material  was founded in Taoyuan in 1992 and started as a conventional FR-4 copper clad thin
core laminate and prepreg manufacturer. Over time, EMC has established its global presence as the

world's largest supplier of halogen-free high-speed CCLs with its leading technology expertise. In
2025, infrastructure applications, including servers, switches, and LEO satellite accounted for 65-
70% of revenue, followed by mobile devices (20-25%), and automation/industrial/others (5-10%).

Company Description

### Page 3

Elite Material UBS Research
business overview
Founded in 1992, EMC  started as a manufacturer of conventional FR-4 CCL and
prepregs used in printed circuit boards. The company began moving up the value chain
with its entry into HDI in 1997, followed by  the introduction of halogen-free materials
licensed from Hitachi Chemical in 2002, allowing EMC to establish a leading position in
handset HDI applications. Smartphones subsequently became EMC's core franchise with

the company sharing c70% market share and becoming a key supplier to Apple as well
as leading Android brands.
EMC began its next major product transition in 2017, when it entered the high-speed
digital materials market with ~0% market share. Over the following seven years, the
company expanded its low-loss material portfolio and customer qualifications across
high-speed switches, general servers and cloud infrastructure, before benefiting from
the rapid growth of AI servers and CSP ASIC platforms. EMC’s high-speed digital market

share reached 28% in 2023, when it became the global No.1 supplier, and increased
further to 38.6% in 2025. This transition has materially reshaped its revenue mix:
infrastructure increased from around 30% of revenue in 2019-21 to 75-80% currently,
while handset exposure declined from 50% to 10-15%. EMC’s customer base has
consequently broadened from handset-centric exposure toward leading networking
and cloud customers, including NVIDIA-related AI platforms and major CSP ASIC

programs. Building on its HDI expertise, EMC further expanded into IC substrate
materials in 2020, and has grown its market share to 1.6% by 2025 where it is now the
only non-Japanese/Korean player with substrate material technology expertise.

HDI leadership underpins EMC’s longstanding consumer-tech franchise
EMC has historically been the global leader in mobile-device applications, with 70%

market share in mobile-phone CCL and over 80% in 5G smartphone CCL. Mobile
devices remain a meaningful 10-15% of EMC's Q326 sales, supported by  its
longstanding leadership in HDI materials, where it has ranked No.1 globally for more
than 20 years with over 70% share. EMC began developing conventional HDI in 1997,
followed by Anylayer HDI in 2010 and mSAP in 2017, progressively enabling finer lines
and higher circuit density. Unlike high-layer-count (HLC) boards, which primarily add

layers to accommodate more circuitry, HDI uses microvias, finer routing and sequential
lamination to pack more interconnects into a smaller board area, making it particularly
well suited to smartphones, tablets and notebooks where miniaturization is critical. The
repeated lamination and finer structures also place greater demands on CCL, including
dimensional stability, resin-flow control, low CTE, CAF resistance and adhesion, creating
meaningful materials know-how rather than simply a different PCB process. This

expertise helped EMC establish an early position in the Apple supply chain, when
handheld devices rose to  50% of sales in 2016.  EMC’s  historical smartphone customer
base has included Apple, Samsung, Huawei, HTC and Nokia. While consumer tech is no
longer EMC's growth focus, we view it as a stable and strategically important revenue
base, complementing the companies rapidly expanding infrastructure focus.

### Page 4

Figure 3: EMC's technology advancements
Source: Company data, courtesy of EMC
Riding the AI-driven CCL upgrade cycle, with M9 extending high-end leadership
AI infrastructure has become EMC’s core growth engine, with infrastructure reaching
roughly 75-80% of revenue in Q326 and growing 183% YoY, driven primarily by
accelerating AI server and high-speed networking demand. EMC has translated this
demand into a leading position in high-end materials, capturing 38.6% of the global

high-speed halogen-free CCL market in 2025, which should enable the company to
continue outgrowing the broader market through both share gains and content
expansion. Industry forecasts cited by the company call for high-end CCL market to
grow at a 40% CAGR in 2024-2027F and AI CCL at a 178% CAGR in 2025-2027F, while
UBS expects hyperscaler capex to grow +96%/+74% YoY in 2026/27E. As AI spending
broadens across both  GPUs and ASICs, we expect EMC to benefit from rising system

volumes as well as increasing CCL content and value per system.
The latter is driven by a structural migration toward higher-grade, lower-loss materials as
data rates and PCB complexity increase. M7-and-above extreme/super low-loss products
already account for 65-70% of EMC's revenue in Q326, with management expecting
the mix to reach over 70% in 2027. Based on our industry checks, the economic value
creation is non-linear across the material stack. While M7 typically carries a 15-50% ASP

premium over M6, M8 pricing is roughly twice that of M7. The largest step-up occurs at
M9, where ASPs are approximately 5-6x those of M6 and GMs exceed 50% versus
roughly 25-30% for M6 and over 30% for M7. Consequently, EMC's earnings sensitivity
to high-end mix expansion is substantially greater than revenue growth alone would
imply.  M9 represents the next leg of this upgrade cycle. EMC was the first supplier to
complete customer qualification from NVIDIA and expects volume shipments to begin in

H226. Management expects M9 to contribute a mid- to high-single-digit percentage of
revenue in H226 before reaching double digits in 2027. EMC is still the first source for
M9 grade applications  including selected NVIDIA Rubin  boards and next-generation
hyperscaler ASIC platforms, the competitors include potential second source Shengyi
and ITEQ who have also passed qualifications.
Nvidia: sustaining a strong position across successive platform upgrades. EMC’s

position in AI CCL was established in 2023, when it became the primary supplier of high-
speed CCL for Nvidia’s H100 GPU server boards. We believe EMC’s prior track record in
high-end HDI helped position it well as GPU boards moved toward greater layer counts,
multiple lamination and more demanding signal-integrity requirements. Nvidia's
architecture has evolved since then and the supplier landscape has become more
differentiated by board. For GB200/GB300, Doosan is the primary compute-tray CCL

supplier, while EMC is the main supplier for the switch tray, alongside Shengyi. Moving
into Vera Rubin, EMC is positioned for CPX/midplane and switch-tray applications, while
Doosan remains dominant in the compute tray. We believe its over 50% share in
NVIDIA's server CCL should remain stable as it benefits from the annual cadence
upgrades.

### Page 5

ASIC: broad hyperscaler exposure reduces reliance on Nvidia. EMC holds roughly
70% share of ASIC-server CCL, supported by exposure across leading hyperscaler
programs. Beyond Nvidia, EMC is benefiting from the continued ramp of custom silicon
at AWS, Google and Meta, alongside a migration to higher-specification materials from
M7 to M8/M8+. Key growth drivers include AWS Trainium 3, Google TPU v8 shipments
from H226, and expanding deployments of Meta's in-house accelerators. With multiple

custom-silicon platforms scaling concurrently, we expect ASIC demand to remain a
meaningful contributor through 2027, further broadening EMC's participation in
hyperscaler AI spending.
Switch: 800G/1.6T migration drives multi-year upgrade cycle
Switch-related materials account for roughly 15-20% of EMC’s infrastructure revenue.
As the connectivity backbone of AI clusters and cloud data centers, switches are seeing
rapidly rising bandwidth requirements, driving the transition from 400G toward 800G

and ultimately 1.6T. 400G remains the largest contributor today, while 800G began
ramping in 2025 and is expected to take around three years to become mainstream, and
1.6T adoption should begin in 2H26 on a similar trajectory. We expect these overlapping
upgrade cycles to support both volume growth and richer material content over the next
several years.
LEO satellite adds a niche but scalable high-speed CCL opportunity
LEO satellite has developed into a meaningful niche within EMC’s infrastructure

portfolio, accounting for roughly 6% of 2025 revenue, providing  materials across
satellite equipment, ground receiving stations, and user terminal equipment. EMC
began targeting the segment in 2023 and entered mass production for LEO satellite
materials in 2024, alongside completion of next-generation product qualification. The
opportunity fits well with EMC’s existing strengths in high-speed, low-loss laminates
and HDI, as satellite boards require strong signal integrity, thermal reliability and

compact, high-density designs.  Demand is supported by continued constellation
deployment from two major US customers, while material specifications are also
upgrading from M6 to M7 as transmission speeds increase, providing additional CCL
content per satellite. Key competitors include US-based Isola which has an established
presence in aerospace and satellite-grade high-performance laminates. We  see LEO as a
relatively small but differentiated growth driver that broadens EMC’s infrastructure

exposure beyond AI servers and switches.
Substrates represent an emerging growth leg with significant share gain
potential
Substrates remain a relatively small part of Elite Material's business today, but the
company has steadily expanded its market share since entering the market in 2020 to
1.6% in 2025. IC substrate laminates carry significantly higher technical barriers than
conventional PCB laminates, with demanding qualification requirements and a

concentrated supplier base historically dominated by Japanese and Korean vendors
including Resonac, Mitsubishi Gas Chemical (MGC), and Doosan  etc. Ongoing substrate
material shortages have led several customers to qualify alternative suppliers, creating
an opportunity for EMC to penetrate programs supported by leveraging its longstanding
HDI and mSAP expertise as a natural pathway into substrate materials. We believe the
company is currently qualifying materials for AI ASIC-related substrate applications, with

initial shipments potentially commencing following customer approval. Looking
forward, EMC's first milestone is to reach 5% substrate market share, followed by 10%,
with both targets expected to be achieved within the next 12-24 months. We believe
successful execution against these milestones would establish substrates as a
meaningful incremental growth driver and further expand EMC's exposure to AI
infrastructure content growth.

### Page 6

EMC consists of three main business segments: Infrastructure, Mobile Devices, and
Automotive/Industrial.
1. Infrastructure (75-80% of Q326 revenue):
 AI Server (55-60% of Infrastructure)
 General Server (15-20% of Infrastructure)
 Switch (15-20% of Infrastructure)
 LEO satellite (5-10% of Infrastructure)
2. Mobile Devices (10-15% of Q326 revenue). Mobile device applications include
smartphones & tablets, notebooks and memory modules. These products utilize

HDI PCB designs to support device miniaturization, higher circuit density and high-
speed signal transmission.
3. Automotive/Industrial (5-10% of Q326 revenue). Automotive and industrial
applications include autonomous driving (ADAS), electric vehicles (power
conversion and charging), medical devices and industrial electronics, which require
high-reliability PCB materials for demanding operating environments.
Ongoing capacity expansions across Taiwan, Mainland China and Malaysia, and

a unique US production presence
EMC was founded in Taoyuan, Taiwan in 1992, where its headquarters and original CCL/
prepreg manufacturing base remain today. In 2005, EMC added its Hsinchu plant to
provide mass-lamination services for PCB customers requiring outsourced capacity. The
company has since built three major production sites in China, Kunshan in 1999,
Zhongshan in 2005, and Huangshi in 2019, serving Northern/Eastern, Southern and
Central China, respectively. In 2024, EMC established its Penang, Malaysia plant, its first

production base in Southeast Asia. In the US, EMC acquired Arlon EMD in 2020, making
it the only Asian CCL supplier with an active US production presence. In July 2026, EMC
raised planned investment in the site to US$69mn, with the expanded facility targeted
to commence operations from H226.
Management has laid out a substantial multi-year capacity expansion plan to capture
robust AI infrastructure demand and sustained tightness in high-end CCL. In Oct 2026,

the board approved an additional ~NT$98.6bn capex budget for phased factory
expansion across Taiwan, Mainland China and Malaysia, underscoring the scale of
EMC’s capacity build-out. Laminate capacity is expected to increase from 5.85mn
sheets/month in 2026E to 8.70mn in 2027E (+49% YoY), 10.45mn in 2028E (+20%
YoY), 11.95mn in 2029E (+14% YoY) and 15.25mn by 2030E (+28% YoY), implying a
~27% CAGR over 2026-30E.
EMC has nevertheless adjusted the phasing of certain expansion plans as it encountered

longer-than-expected expansion lead times in Taiwan. Most notably, the initial 300k
sheets/month Guanyin addition, previously targeted for Dec 2026, has been pushed
back by ~6 months due to labor/material constraints and longer government safety
inspections, while a further 600k sheets/month addition planned for 2028 has shifted to
2029. By contrast, capacity expansion at Kunshan, China can proceed more quickly.
Demand visibility remains robust, with signed LTAs exceeding 9.4mn sheets of

incremental capacity and supporting full utilization through 2028. Customers are
increasingly entering multi-year LTAs to secure high-end CCL supply, while management
continues to secure LTAs and verbal commitments beyond 2028 and sees customer
demand extending through 2030.

Shareholder structure:
Publicly listed on the Taiwan Stock Exchange in Nov 1998, EMC is chaired by Ting-Yu
Tung who directly  holds ~1.5% of outstanding shares. Related investment vehicles Yu
Chang Investment and Yu Sheng Investment together hold ~10.7%, bringing the
chairman/family-related holding to 12%. FINI ownership peaked at ~55% in 2017 amid
strong smartphone/HDI demand, before declining on smartphone weakness and Apple
SLP share loss. It bottomed at ~24% in 2024 amid concerns over Blackwell CCL share

loss to Korean competitors, but has since recovered to ~41% (vs Taiex's FINI holding
47%), supported by stronger-than-expected AI server/ASIC and high-end CCL demand.

Elite Material UBS Research
Financial analysis
Q326 earnings beat on strong GM expansion and high-end mix. EMC delivered
record-high Q326 revenue of NT$60.9bn, up 29% QoQ and 142% YoY, driven by
strong infrastructure demand, price increases and favorable product mix. Gross margin
expanded 3.8ppts QoQ to 37.7%, with pricing and mix each contributing roughly half
of the improvement, while operating margin reached 30.7% and EPS rose to NT$40.47.
Infrastructure remained the core growth engine at 75-80% of revenue, with sales up

32% QoQ/176% YoY, while  full utilization underscored continued supply-demand
tightness amid strong high-end CCL demand.
Q426 to see continued pricing and mix momentum. For Q426E, EMC guided for
15-20% QoQ revenue growth and 38.0-39.5% GM, supported by continued price
increases and favorable product mix. By application, management expects infrastructure
revenue to grow ~20% QoQ, mobile to remain flattish, and automotive/industrial/
others to decline 5-10% QoQ, with the latter reflecting lower volumes as capacity is

reallocated toward high-speed CCL. We forecast Q426 revenue of NT$69.4bn (+14%
QoQ), GM to increase 0.5ppts to 38.2% and EPS of NT$47.03 (+16% QoQ). EMC plans
5-20% price increases across its portfolio in Q4, with pricing reflecting raw-material cost
increases; management also noted further upside to pricing if the supply-demand
imbalance persists.

Revenue and GM and EPS forecasts for 2027/2028E
We forecast EMC’s revenue to grow 80%/45% YoY in 2027/2028E, supported by
substantial capacity expansion, continued pricing and sustained high-end CCL demand.
Capacity is expected to increase ~49%/20% YoY in 2027/2028, with new capacity
coming online progressively and management expecting utilization to remain full

through 2028. EMC is accelerating investment to capture this demand, with capex rising
from NT$28bn in 2026 to NT$53bn in both 2027 and 2028, although FCF is expected to
remain negative for two years before turning positive in 2028-2029. Management
indicated signed LTAs already exceed 9.4mn sheets of incremental capacity, providing
strong visibility into the ramp, while substrate laminate represents additional upside,
with a new ASIC project expected to complete qualification by end-2026 and begin

shipments in 2027.
Pricing and product mix should provide additional growth on top of the capacity ramp.
EMC expects high-end CCL tightness to persist for the next two years, as extended
equipment lead times constrain the pace of new supply, while E-glass tightness could

### Page 9

persist until H227. Management sees further room for price increases if the supply-
demand imbalance persists. Product mix should provide another ASP and margin
tailwind, with M7+ expected to rise from 65-70% of Q326 sales to 70-80% in 2027. M9
began shipments in H226 and is expected to enter MP in 2027, while M10 qualification
is targeted for completion by end-2026, enabling initial shipments in H127 ahead of MP
in H227. With  typical generational upgrades carrying at least a 30% price increase, we

forecast GM to expand from 35.7% in 2026E to 40.5%/42.0% in 2027/2028E and
OPM to rise from 28.6% to 34.2%/36.2%, reflecting continued pricing, richer high-end
mix and operating leverage, driving EPS of NT$280.82/430.74, up 116%/53% YoY,
respectively. Our 2027/2028E EPS estimates are c.4%/4% below consensus, reflecting
more conservative utilization and pricing assumptions to account for potential downside
from slower AI/NVIDIA demand, while we remain positive on EMC’s technology

leadership and high-end product positioning.

Sensitivity analysis
Quantifying earnings downside  risks if AI spending slows down from 2028
The US Department of Commerce measures announced on 16 November seek to
prevent China AI-chip controls from being circumvented through third-country data

centers. The rules extend controls beyond the physical shipment of advanced chips to
who ultimately accesses the compute, while introducing licensing requirements for
advanced-chip exports, re-exports and transfers to Singapore, Malaysia, Indonesia and
Thailand, as well as Brazil, which BIS identifies as presenting higher-than-normal
diversion risk. Chips destined for US-headquartered companies or US-parented
subsidiaries are exempt from this country-level requirement, subject to reporting

conditions. The rules do not altogether prohibit ASEAN AI investment, but raise licensing
and compliance hurdles for parts of the data center ecosystem (LINK).  This raises
concerns on first-order impacts of likely to be delayed GPU deployment and slower data
center build-outs. While we believe AI data center build-out remains supported by cloud
computing power growth, enterprise AI and inference, we note it is constrained by data-
sovereignty and latency requirements, as well as power and land supply limits. We

### Page 10

believe the market continues to expect a strong 2027 but is now more cautious for
2028. We think some market expectations of a down year might be too conservative as
the UBS team has been noting that operating cash flow should support sustainable
capex for 2028. That being said, we still see some sensitivity around potential acts that
might cap hyperscalers from securing permitted sites and/or receiving power/water
permits.
Below we examine the potential downside risks into 2028 earnings if AI spending

materially slows down vs our current base case scenario. Our key assumptions and
findings are as follows:
 If capacity expansion plan falls short of expectations...: The company has
announced plans to expand CCL monthly capacity from 4.95mn sheets in 2025 to
10.45mn/15.25mn sheets by 2028F/2030F. Our current model assumes its CCL
monthly capacity of 10.08mn sheets in 2028E, and note that if capacity additions
were only 50%/30%/20%/10% executed compared to our base case scenario,

this will imply potential 2028E earnings downside of 25%/35%/39%/43% to our
current estimates.
 ...and pricing environment becomes less favourable: We currently expect to
see AI CCL content value increases driven by computing and networking/
interconnect demands; and extended equipment lead time and qualification
process along with increasing manufacturing challenges are also suggesting more
constrained upstream inputs. That being said, if pricing power starts to weaken on

AI spending slowdown, assuming pricing falls short of our current estimates by
5%/10%/15%/20%, on top of lowered capacity, this will imply potential earnings
downside of 32%/45%/53%/60% to our current 2028E estimates.

EMC Share price EMC P/E (x) +1 Std Dev =20x
EMC Avg P/E  14x -1 Std Dev =7x
EMC P/E (x)EMC share price (NT$)
Source: Refinitiv Eikon
EMC’s 12-month forward P/E historically traded below 20x prior to 2023, before re-

rating with the onset of the AI cycle. The re-rating accelerated from 2025 through H126,
driven by 1) EMC’s leadership in M8 and expectations that this would extend into M9; 2)
growing exposure to hyperscaler ASIC programmes and expectations of regaining a
leading supplier position in NVIDIA’s Rubin platform; and 3) rising CCL content per
system as AI architectures adopt higher-specification materials and more complex board
designs. Expectations for CCL price increases and EMC’s technology and capacity

advantages provided further support in H126, taking its forward P/E to a historical peak
of 47x in April 2026. Since the AI cycle began, EMC has traded within a roughly 10-48x
forward P/E range, averaging around 20x.
The subsequent de-rating has been equally significant. EMC’s share price has fallen 56%
from its August peak, versus TAIEX down 21%, amid broader tech-sector selling, rising
concerns over slower AI spending, and uncertainty over whether PTFE adoption in

NVIDIA’s next-generation platforms could challenge EMC's high-end CCL positioning.
At NT$2,810 as of 20 November 2026, EMC trades at 10x/7x our 2027E/2028E EPS,
below its long-term historical median of 13.5x and well below its c.20x post-2023
average. Importantly, our 2027/2028E EPS estimates are already c.4%/4% below
consensus, partly reflecting a more conservative view amid rising AI demand concerns.
We believe the current valuation largely reflects these concerns, but gives insufficient

credit to EMC’s sustained technology leadership and earnings growth from continued
high-end material migration. Management expects M7+ to reach 70-80% of sales in
2027, versus 65-70% in Q326, while M9 is expected to enter mass production in 2027
and M10 could begin initial shipments in H127.
Our NT$5,000 price target is based on 12x 2028E P/E, at the mid to low end of EMC’s
7-20x historical valuation range. This compares with 8x for TUC versus its 7-17x

historical range and 10x for ITEQ versus its 9-19x historical range. We believe EMC’s
premium is warranted by its high-end material leadership, broader GPU and hyperscaler
ASIC exposure, and technology and capacity advantages. Our target multiple is also
above the 8x average for other CCL suppliers and 7x for PCB suppliers, while closer to
the 10x average for substrate suppliers as EMC expands its substrate CCL business.
EMC is trading at NT$2,810 (as of 20

Nov 2026)

UPSIDE (NT$9,000): We assume 2028E sales growth of 49.3%, driven by 62% CCL

growth, reflecting faster M9/M10 adoption, stronger AI server/switch demand and
sustained pricing. We assume a gross margin of 42.5% and EPS of NT$450.2. We apply
20x 2028E P/E to derive our NT$9,000 upside valuation.
BASE (NT$5,000): We assume 2028E sales growth of 45.0%, driven by 57% CCL
growth, supported by continued AI demand, high-end material upgrades and capacity
expansion. We assume a gross margin of 42.0% and EPS of NT$430.7. We apply 12x

2028E P/E to derive our NT$5,000 base-case valuation.
DOWNSIDE (NT$1,650): We assume 2028E sales growth of 17.2%, driven by 22% CCL
growth, reflecting slower AI server/switch demand, delayed high-end material adoption
and an earlier-than-expected easing in CCL supply tightness. We assume a gross margin
of 36.8% and EPS of NT$253.8. We apply 7x 2028E P/E to derive our NT$1,650
downside valuation.

### Page 14

Elite Material UBS Research
Company Description
Company Description
Elite Material was founded in Taoyuan in 1992 as a conventional
FR-4 copper-clad thin-core laminate and prepreg manufacturer.
Over time, EMC expanded globally to become the world's largest
supplier of halogen-free high-speed CCLs, underpinned by its
leading technology expertise. In 2025, infrastructure
applications, including servers, switches, and LEO satellites,
accounted for 65-70% of revenue, followed by mobile devices

(20-25%), and automation, industrial and other applications (5-
10%).
Industry Outlook
The CCL industry outlook remains positive, supported by AI
server investment and upgrades to high-speed networking
infrastructure. Larger, higher-layer-count PCBs and tighter signal-
integrity requirements are increasing demand for high-
performance, ultra-low-loss laminates, supporting both material
content growth and a richer product mix. Tight supply of
specialty glass fabric and other inputs is supporting laminate

price increases, although rising material costs and supply
constraints remain risks to margins and shipment growth. These
trends should favour suppliers with advanced material
capabilities and reliable access to critical inputs.

Company Description
Elite Material was founded in Taoyuan in 1992 as a conventional FR-4 copper-clad thin-core
laminate and prepreg manufacturer. Over time, EMC expanded globally to become the
world's largest supplier of halogen-free high-speed CCLs, underpinned by its leading
technology expertise. In 2025, infrastructure applications, including servers, switches, and

LEO satellites, accounted for 65-70% of revenue, followed by mobile devices (20-25%), and
automation, industrial and other applications (5-10%).
Valuation Method and Risk Statement
We value EMC using a PE-based methodology.
Key risks include: 1) slower-than-expected high-end CCL adoption and AI infrastructure
deployments or delays in server upgrade cycles; 2) delays in capacity expansion or production
ramp-up; 3) stronger-than-expected competition and market-share losses in high-end CCL;

and 4) higher raw-material costs and weaker-than-expected cost pass-through to customers.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

大銀微系統(4576.TT) 
產業：電機機械 
受惠半導體設備擴充潮，2027 年營收成⻑將⾼於 20%  
 投資評價與建議 
高毛利率的奈微米定位平台營收比重已近60%，產品結構及附加價值提升帶動
毛利率提升，技術升級、集團分工與產能區域化佈局將帶動大銀微2027年營收
成⻑高於20%。 
 重點評論分析 
3Q26營收年增77%，4Q26營收可望與3Q26持平或略⾼：亞洲(台灣除外)、
台灣、美洲及歐洲地區3Q26分別年增89%、101%、127%及-12%，全球半導
體供應鏈重組與在地化採購需求強勁，3Q26營收12.2億元季增19%(年增
77%)，高階定位平台出貨佔比提升及規模經濟發揮，毛利率42.4%季增
5.3pp(年增8.6pp)，規模經濟及費用控制得宜下，營業利益率24.3%季增
6.4pp(年增13.9pp)；稅前淨利3.3億元季增113%(年增255%)，EPS 2.15元，
1~3Q26EPS 4.07元；4Q26營收可望與3Q26持平或略高，毛利率將維持在
40%上下。 
發展策略將持續朝向⾼附加價值市場，2027年營收成⻑將⾼於20%：未來的
發展策略將持續朝向高附加價值市場邁進，包括發展精密力量控制技術（涵蓋
先進封裝Hybrid Bonding、高速固晶機等應用），大銀微將專注於系統整合
銷售，不單獨販售E1控制器，以確保軟硬體深度綁定，帶動營收與獲利持續同
步雙位數成⻑，2027年營收成⻑高於20%。 
 投資風險 
同業殺價競爭、地緣政治、匯率波動。

| 營運概況 
3Q26毛利率及營益率均大增

資料來源 : CMoney 
3Q26營收年增77%，毛利率及營益率均大增：亞洲(台灣除外)、台灣、美洲及歐洲地區3Q26分別年增89%、101%、
127%及-12%，全球半導體供應鏈重組與在地化採購需求強勁，3Q26營收12.2億元季增19%(年增77%)，高階定位平台
出貨佔比提升及規模經濟發揮，毛利率42.4%季增5.3pp(年增8.6pp)，規模經濟及費用控制得宜下，營業利益率24.3%
季增6.4pp(年增13.9pp)；稅前淨利3.3億元季增113%(年增255%)，EPS 2.15元，1~3Q26EPS 4.07元；毛利率提升主
要是產品結構及附加價值提升，奈微米定位平台由於高度客製化、整合空氣軸承、花崗岩平台、多軸線性馬達與高階雷
射補償演算法，且直接對接半導體⼀線客戶，毛利率達40~50%；精密運動及控制元件過去單賣馬達線圈與磁軌毛利率
未及30%，但大銀微力推「馬達＋自製E1系列驅動器＋控制器」整套搭配銷售，加上高單價晶圓移載用直驅力矩馬達出
貨放量，使元件部門毛利率亦同步墊高至30~35%。 
4Q26營收可望與3Q26持平或略⾼，毛利率將維持在40%上下： 4Q26營收可望與3Q26持平或略高，毛利率將維持在
40%上下，目前高階平台訂單能見度達5~6個月，交期已縮短至3個月；元件類能見度約3個月，交期約2~2.5個月，大
銀微具備85%的關鍵零組件自製率（包含線性馬達、驅動器、控制器），能快速打樣並提供高彈性交期。 
發展策略將持續朝向⾼附加價值市場，2027年營收成⻑將⾼於20%：未來的發展策略將持續朝向高附加價值市場邁進，
包括發展精密力量控制技術（涵蓋先進封裝Hybrid Bonding、高速固晶機等應用），大銀微將專注於系統整合銷售，不
單獨販售E1控制器，以確保軟硬體深度綁定，帶動營收與獲利持續同步雙位數成⻑，2027年營收成⻑高於20%；隨著AI

與「大型花崗岩空氣軸承系統」，大銀微在此領域具備完整大尺寸平台調校經驗，單機採用金額較傳統封裝設備提升2~3

大銀微ODM，訂單能見度已直達2Q27；全球AI伺服器主板與交換器板廠在泰國大舉擴產，帶動高階雷射鑽孔機、機械
鑽孔機與成型機對大銀微線性馬達與高階驅動器的強勁拉貨；配合上銀(2049)集團佈局AI人型機器人，大銀微的高轉矩
密度無框力矩馬達與微型驅動器，具備切入機器人旋轉關節與靈巧⼿電控系統之⻑期潛力。 
CPO 相關營收訂單能見度已達 2H27：AI 資料中心算力互連由銅線轉向光學互連（800G/1.6T 光模組與 CPO），光
纖陣列（FAU）與矽光晶片波導進行主動對位（Active Alignment）時，要求定位精度必須達到奈米級且兼具極高反
應速度與抗震性，大銀微系統能解決高倍率顯微鏡檢測時的微小震動干擾，有效提升客戶設備產出率，主要應用於光
學引擎測試與系統級檢測，優勢在於可提供高精密、輕薄且體積緊湊的六軸定位平台，E1 控制器能將伺服穩定度提升
至正負 1 奈米，由於大銀的線性馬達是完全自行研發，因此可做到比業界體積更小，且可做到機電整合；大銀微開發
之多自由度奈米定位平台已切入台系與美系矽光子檢測及封裝設備鏈，2Q26 起開始交貨國內矽光子檢測設備大廠，
2H26 進⼀步放量，訂單能見度已達 2H27。 
2027 年資本支出為 1.2~1.5 億元，將新增 20~30%產能：面對全球半導體供應鏈區域化（美、日、歐晶圓廠擴建）
以及 AI 伺服器帶動高階 PCB/載板廠大舉移往泰國與東南亞設廠，大銀微採取「核心奈米平台留台灣台中擴產、標準
控制元件與次系統模組強化亞洲及歐美前線組裝」的雙軌策略，2H26 台中廠區針對高階微奈米定位系統與精密運動元
件已啟動擴產計畫，擴產產能規劃於 4Q26~1Q27 陸續完成，屆時將增加 20~30%的產能，進⼀步⽀撐 4Q26~1Q27
的出貨順暢度；第⼀階段產能擴建已完成，第二階段擴產預計於 2027 年 2 月底至 3 月中旬完成，並已開始規劃第三
階段，2027 年資本⽀出為 1.2~1.5 億元。 
微奈米定位系統營收佔比逐步攀⾼

資料來源 : 大銀微，玉山投顧整理及預估、繪製

| 大銀微的定位與優勢 
大銀微掌握直驅馬達、線性馬達與驅動控制技術：大銀微系統成立於1997年，為上銀集團旗下的機電與超精密定位核心
企業，在集團分工體系中，⺟公司上銀（2049）負責提供高剛性機械結構與精密線性傳動部件，而大銀微則負責提供「大
腦（控制器）」、「神經（驅動器）」與「直接驅動心臟（線性馬達、力矩馬達、音圈馬達）」，大銀微與上銀為機電
整合雙引擎，大銀微掌握直驅馬達、線性馬達與驅動控制技術，而上銀專精於奈米與微米級的機械傳動（螺桿、線軌、
減速機）；大銀微的優勢包括馬達與驅動器與控制器「三合⼀」全自製能力(可針對奈米級平台進行底層演算法調校)、

### Page 4

4 
集團垂直整合綜效(結合上銀高精度螺桿/滑軌與自⾝線性馬達，提供⼀站式機電整合⽅案)、已通過全球前三大半導體設
備商及晶圓代工龍頭嚴苛認證。 
產業現狀：日本政府禁止高階工具機出口中國大陸，而中國大陸也管制高精度工具機出口，在全球貿易壁壘高築下，包
括北美、東南亞訂單大舉湧向台灣工具機業者；惟台灣工具機整機廠高階市場現面臨日本工具機廠挾日圓貶值優勢的威
脅，中低階或泛用型標準機又遭逢大陸業者低價搶市的威脅。 
產品應用範圍涵蓋半導體設備、面板及光電與自動化及PCB與載板設備等：大銀微的產品應用範圍涵蓋半導體設備(佔比
逾40%)、面板及光電與自動化（佔比約30~35%）、PCB與載板設備（佔比約20%）、精密量測及自動化、生技醫療等
產業，重量級客戶有應材（AMAT）、艾斯摩爾（ASML）、科磊（KLAC）及東京威力科創(8035.T)等全球前四大半導
體設備製造廠。

| 機器人產業簡介 
服務型機器人佔比較⾼，人形機器人正在起步：依據Precedence Research預估，服務型機器人市場2026年為725億美
元，2035年將可增加至2,338億美元，年均複合成⻑率為14%；工業型機器人市場2026年為307億美元，2035年將可增
加至933億美元，年均複合成⻑率為13%；人形機器人市場2026年為22億美元，2035年將可增加至88億美元，年均複
合成⻑率為35%，人形機器人雖然市場成⻑速度較快，但由於正在起步，市場規模及滲透率仍較低。 
機器人市場

資料來源 : Precedence Research、 玉山投顧整理、繪製 
人形機器人的結構與零組件

資料來源 : IEK、 玉山投顧整理、繪製

### Page 5

5 
AI模型讓機器人可持續學習優化：機器人智能可分為三個決策層、執行層及感知層三個主要層級，決策層負責環境理解、
任務規劃與行為決策；執行層由機器人本體與運動控制系統組成，包括伺服馬達、減速機、關節模組與控制器，負責把 
AI 的決策轉換成具體動作；感知層負責蒐集環境資訊，常見設備有RGB或RGB-D攝影機、雷射測距儀、力矩與觸覺感
測器，以及慣性量測單元，透過多模態感測資訊融合，系統才能對環境與物件有更精準的理解；AI模型讓機器人可持續
學習優化，隨著人形機器人與雙臂操作機器人快速發展，機構設計也愈來愈接近人類，如多自由度關節、靈巧⼿等設計，
讓機器人能在既有的人類工作環境中執行更複雜的任務。 
機器人智能系統主要層級包括決策層、執行層與感知層

資料來源 : 台達電 
控制器相當於機器人的大腦，AI時代更形重要：控制器的主要功能包括運動控制(計算多軸反向運動學，實現精準定位與
軌跡追蹤)、信號傳遞(將邏輯決策轉為電氣信號傳給驅動器與馬達)、安全防護(緊急停止與安全監控保護人員與設備)與
系統整合(連接視覺、力覺感測器以及工廠聯網通訊) ，AI時代將更形重要。 
AI時代控制器更形重要

資料來源 : 新代法說會簡報 
人機協作為目前工廠新典型，未來將走向智慧化：人機協作是指人類與人工智慧、機器人或自動化系統共同工作、互補
優勢以達成任務的模式，它透過機器處理大量數據與重複性工作，結合人類的創造力、同理心與終端決策，實現效率與
價值的最大化；核心優勢與分工在人機協作的模式下，人類與機器各司其職，發揮最強效能，機器擅⻑資料快速運算、
例行程序自動化、預測分析與24小時監控，而人類擅⻑戰略⽅向決策、複雜情境判斷、創意發想以及人際同理心溝通；
人機協作的共同效益為大幅降低人力閒置、提升組織生產力，並協助員工從低技術工作轉型為高附加價值工作，人機協
作為目前工廠新典型，但未來將走向智慧化，智慧化機器人結合了AI、先進感測器與精密控制系統，能自主感知周遭環
境、進行即時資料分析、做出決策，並執行原本需要人類智慧與物理動作才能完成的複雜任務，將AI實體化，其核心技

### Page 6

6 
術與特點在於將大腦演算法與實體機械結合，讓機器人具備空間理解與動態適應能力，並具有高精度感測與視覺，可透
過3D深度相機、光達與扭力感測器，做到精準避障與靈敏控制，由於具有模組化關節與驅動，透過高度整合馬達、驅動
器與通訊介面，提升扭力密度與即時反應速度；智慧化機器人主要應用領域智慧製造包括彈性產線、自動化組裝、設備
巡檢與物料搬運；醫療與照護⽅面如⽀援⼿術輔助、醫療物流與高齡化社會的照護陪伴；也能應用於特殊與極端環境，
如進入災害現場、地下空間或危險區域進行偵察與救援。 
協作型機器人市場2034年預估達713億美元

資料來源 : Precedence Research、 玉山投顧整理、繪製 
協作型機器人市場至2034年的年均複合成⻑率為35%：協作型機器人（Cobot）是⼀種專為與人類在共同工作空間內安
全協作而設計的機器人，與傳統工業機器人不同，其設計核心不是「最大化速度與力量」，而是「讓機器能夠安全地在
人的旁邊工作」，協作型機器人的概念最早在 1990 年代由學術界提出，2008年丹麥Universal Robots推出第⼀款商
業化Cobot協作型機器人後，這個品類才真正進入工業應用視野。此後十餘年間，隨著感測技術、控制演算法與製造成
本的成熟，協作型機器人快速普及，從歐美大廠擴展到亞洲製造業的中小型工廠。依據Precedence Research預估，協
作型機器人市場2025年為56億美元，2034年將可增加至713億美元，年均複合成⻑率為35%。 
機器人走向通用化、智能化、自主化

Topics  Regulatory reform in India's insurance sector

Over the past quarter century, the Indian government has pursued the liberalization of the country's insurance market, 
with the aim of bringing stability to people's living standards and strengthening financial intermediation functions. 
Recently, however, it has begun to reverse course, tightening rules in order to better protect consumers.

■ India's insurance market has grown rapidly with deregulation 
The Indian government has long pursued 
the development of the country's insurance 
market, with the aim of bringing stability to 
people's living standards and strengthening 
financial intermediation functions. This 
stance can be attributed to two background 
factors:  
The first is that India's social security 
system remains underdeveloped. As a result, 
enrollment in health and life insurance can 
provide a vital safety net for mitigating the 
risk of sharp declines in household income 
due to unexpected illnesses or accidents.  
The second is that the sustainable 
development of the Indian economy requires 
the accumulation of long-term and stable 
domestic capital, separate from short-term 
bank deposits, in order to finance the 
development of infrastructure and the 
nurturing of industry. The hope has been that 
the enormous sums of money collected and 
managed by insurance companies can serve 
as a reliable source for this long-term 
financing.  
These factors have led the Indian 
government to walk a path of gradual 
deregulation over the past quarter century. 
The process began with the liberalization of 
market entry by private and foreign 
companies in 2000, which was followed by 
a lifting of the ban on "bancassurance" (the 
sale of insurance through bank branches) in 
2002, the raising of the ceiling on foreign 
ownership, and other reforms.  
This series of deregulatory measures 
spurred the entry of numerous private and foreign 
firms, causing market competition to intensify. Several 
Japanese insurers, including Nippon Life and the Tokio 
Marine Group, also entered the market, helping to drive 
investment in India.  
Besides deregulation related to market entry and 
sales, other factors also contributed to the rapid growth 
of India's insurance market, namely the expansion of 
the middle class, improvements in household financial 
literacy, and the digitalization of insurance services. 
Premium income grew at a pace exceeding economic 
growth, and in recent years insurance companies' assets 
under management have reached a level equivalent to 
Year Event
Establishment of the IRDAI (Insurance Regulatory
and Development Authority of India)
Acceptance of market entry applications from
domestic private firms and foreign entities
(foreign equity cap set at 26%)
2002
Authorization of bancassurance (sale of insurance
through banks or bank-owned distribution
channels)
2015 Increase in foreign equity cap from 26% to 49%
2016
Introduction of a post-facto notification system
for the sale of new insurance products (replacing
the previous prior-approval system)
2021 Increase in foreign equity cap from 49% to 74%
2022 Rationalization of regulations regarding online and diverse insurance distribution channels
2023
Removal of caps on commissions paid by
insurance companies to agents and
intermediaries

major reforms to the insurance distribution
system

3 
incentives to sell products and inappropriate sales practices. This should shore up the credibility of the 
insurance market and the foundation for its sustainable development. 
On the minus side, there are worries about side effects arising from the transition. As rules on selling 
become stricter, insurance companies and the financial institutions acting as insurance agents will inevitably 
see some temporary moderation in the pace of their earnings growth. Moreover, given that insurance 
companies and financial institutions are major investors in India's financial markets, tighter regulation is 
likely to exert downward pressure on equity and bond prices. 
These changes in the insurance market provide an important perspective for assessing the future 
trajectory of the Indian economy. They illustrate that rapid market expansion driven by liberalization can 
create a variety of problems. And this dynamic is not limited to the insurance sector, as it can also occur 
across many other industries. At the same time, the redesign and recalibration of regulations in response to 
such developments can provide a foundation for a new stage of growth. The new insurance regulations 
could constitute an early example of this phenomenon, so will be closely watched to see what changes they 
bring in the years ahead.  
  (Shotaro Kumagai)

Topics  Talent travel curbs may weigh on China’s growth

In September, the Chinese government introduced new exit-entry rules. Indicative of an effort to retain talent in order 
to prevent technology leakage, the rules could narrow opportunities for China to absorb overseas knowledge and weigh 
on its economic growth over the medium to long term.

■ Scope of overseas travel restrictions expanded 
On September 15, the Chinese 
government brought in new rules 
specifying conditions and screening 
procedures for entry and exit. The new 
rules explicitly state that the Ministry of 
Commerce or other departments may 
prohibit Chinese citizens from leaving the 
country if they violate export-control or 
technology import/export regulations and 
are deemed likely to threaten national 
industrial or technological security.  
Under China's Exit and Entry 
Administration Law, which came into 
effect in 2013, defendants and suspects in 
criminal cases, as well as persons involved 
in unsettled civil cases whom courts have 
prohibited from leaving the country, had 
already been subject to exit restrictions. In 
addition, Chinese citizens could be denied 
permission to leave the country if the 
competent departments determined that 
their departure might endanger national 
security or interests. Aside from formal exit 
bans, personnel who handle state secrets 
and officials in the public sector have long 
been subject to controls such as centralized 
passport management and the need to 
obtain approval in advance of overseas travel. What the new rules effectively do is make it clear that exit 
can also be restricted when there are issues from the standpoint of economic security. 
Against this backdrop, it was reported at the end of September that overseas travel restrictions already 
imposed on key personnel in the AI and semiconductor sectors had been expanded to include their spouses, 
children, and other family members. According to these reports, these key personnel include prominent AI 
startup founders, senior executives at major private enterprises, and core R&D talent. Although the measure 
does not constitute a blanket travel ban, affected individuals and their families are reportedly required to 
obtain government approval for exit even in the case of short private trips or business travel. 
This regulatory tightening is thought to be related to the technological rivalry between the U.S. and China. 
As competition surrounding critical technologies such as generative AI intensifies, the Chinese government 
appears to view not only technologies themselves, but also the researchers and engineers who create them, 
as strategic assets. By widening travel curbs to encompass family members, the authorities are likely 
seeking to prevent overseas emigration by individuals or the relocation of R&D operations abroad. A 
noteworthy aspect of the recent reports is that the controls apparently now extend beyond the public sector 
to key personnel at private enterprises, as well as their families. Restrictions like these on talent mobility 
could undermine the mechanisms of technology absorption and productivity improvement that have 
hitherto facilitated China's growth.

■ China's productivity gains have been supported by market opening and technology 
absorption 
Productivity exerts a substantial influence on 
economic growth. Total factor productivity (TFP), an 
indicator of an economy's overall production 
efficiency, provides an estimate of efficiency in 
production that cannot be explained solely in terms of 
inputs of capital and labor, and reflects factors such as 
levels of technology and efficiency of resource 
allocation. 
China's TFP has been rising for many years. In 
addition to the transition to a market economy and the 
growth of private enterprises since the country adopted 
the policy of reform and opening up, Chinese 
productivity has benefitted from the absorption of 
foreign technology and managerial know‑how through 
trade and direct investment. A labor shift from 
agriculture to manufacturing and other sectors, along 
with competition among firms and the weeding out of 
weaker players, has also helped deliver productivity 
gains across the economy.  
Nevertheless, China's TFP remains low relative to 
other major economies. According to estimates from 
Penn World Table, China's TFP in 2023 was less than 
half that of the U.S., and it was also below the levels not 
only of Germany and other major European economies 
and of South Korea and Japan, but of Thailand and 
Malaysia, too. Factors behind this include inefficient 
resource allocation and the slow diffusion of advanced 
technologies and management practices. Preferential 
financial and institutional treatment for state‑owned 
enterprises, regional protectionism, and mechanisms 
that impede market entry and exit have distorted the 
allocation of resources, preventing capital and labor 
from being fully utilized by more productive firms and sectors. Moreover, although China has achieved 
strong competitiveness in certain advanced industries, such as electric vehicles and semiconductors, the 
leading-edge technologies and management practices developed in these sectors have not sufficiently 
diffused to the service sector and small and medium‑sized enterprises, which form the foundation of the 
economy, and this has constrained overall productivity growth.  
■ Moves to contain technology could hinder growth over the medium to long term 
Efforts to retain talent in order to prevent technology leakage could further delay China's productivity 
catch‑up with advanced economies. While restrictions on overseas travel by elite professionals may help 
with the retention of domestic R&D talent and protection of current technological advantages, they could 
also reduce opportunities to absorb overseas knowledge and to jointly develop new technologies with 
foreign partners. The transfer of technology and know‑how does not occur solely through the sharing of 
"explicit knowledge," in the form of academic papers, technical blueprints, and so on. "Tacit knowledge," 
which is transmitted through face‑to‑face discussion, collaborative work, and the acquisition of experience 
in production settings, is also crucial. If travel controls delay or prevent participation in international 
conferences, joint research projects, and technology exchanges with overseas companies, opportunities to 
absorb such knowledge may be lost, slowing technological innovation and improvements in production 
processes. Furthermore, if obtaining travel approval becomes more time‑consuming or less predictable, the 
overseas assignment or international deployment of technical personnel could also be obstructed. 
The new rules also cover the entry of foreign nationals. If uncertainty surrounding the administration of 
entry and exit controls increases, it could create obstacles to attracting and retaining highly skilled talent 
from abroad. At a time when inefficient resource allocation and the slow diffusion of advanced knowledge 
are already weighing on productivity growth in China, curbs on people-to- people exchange could further

## Document 295: Blue Wave_ Democrats sweep the US midterms

Source PDF: all report pdf/Blue Wave_ Democrats sweep the US midterms.pdf
Report source: 渣打銀行 (Market Watch)
Date: 2026-11-05
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Blue Wave: Democrats 
sweep the US midterms  
Summary 
Event: The Democrats gained control of the House of 
Representatives and the Senate at the US midterm elections 
that took place this week.  
Our View: With the US government now no longer under 
unified control of the Republicans, gridlock has returned to 
Washington. We believe investors should not overreact to 
these results and should stay invested. 
In equities, investors should continue to diversify their 
equity exposure into more defensive sectors. As we 
transition to mid-cycle for the equity markets, we expect 
stocks to continue to deliver positive returns in the 8-10% 
range per annum, a downshift from the torrid pace of gains in 
the earlier part of the cycle.  
In bonds, income remains attractive given recent spread 
widening. We favour non-hyperscaler investment grade 
corporate bonds at the three to seven-year part of the 
curve. We continue to expect the US dollar to remain range-
bound in the near term, as we expect two more rate hikes 
from the Fed, but as this mini rate hike cycle ends in the 
second quarter of 2027, the USD index (DXY) is likely to 
depreciate towards 98.5 as markets start to anticipate a Fed 
rate cut in the second half of next year.  
Oil is likely to rebound in the short-term on heightened 
geopolitical risk, but as supply chains adapt to the current 
situation and Congress exercises oversight on defense 
spending, WTI prices are likely to drift towards our 12-month 
target of USD 80 per barrel.

Democrats made solid gains across the Senate, House 
of Representatives and State Governors 
Results reported as at 7:45PM Singapore Time

Source: The Wall Street Journal, Standard Chartered

### Page 2

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 5 November 2026

Major implications of Democrat control of 
Congress   
1) Fiscal expansionary policies may slow. Democrats are 
now in position to block any new tax cuts or spending plans 
from the Trump administration. At the same time, any fiscal 
policies proposed by Democrats may also face Trump’s veto. 
Hence the fiscal trajectory of the US will likely remain stuck 
over the next two years. As we get into the second half of 
2027, the US debt ceiling will once again loom, and gridlock 
could potentially bring back government shutdowns. 
However, as they have always done in the past, compromise 
will ultimately prevail, in our opinion.  
2) Trump remains in control of foreign policy. With the 
midterms now out of the way, could Trump decide to re-start 
combat operations with Iran? We believe there is a risk that 
US and Israel – now that both countries’ have concluded 
important elections – will once again be tempted to restart 
combat operations to force an end to the conflict. However, 
the duration of any renewed hostilities may be limited by 
Congress next year as the Democrats will likely withhold 
funding for further military operations. In the near-term, oil 
prices may experience renewed volatility before stabilising at 
a lower level next year. The introduction of new tariffs on 
trading partners may prove harder with Democrat oversight. 
3) AI data centre moratoriums could grow, though model 
development regulatory changes would be harder to 
pass. While the headlines have focused on the House and 
Senate results, one important development has also 
occurred at the gubernatorial level. Notably, Democrats have 
won the governor races in Texas and Georgia, two big data 
centre states. As data centre build out permits are largely 
decided at the local state level, this will have important 
ramifications for build out plans next year. However, any 
Democratic proposals to regulate AI model developments 
would need to be signed off by President Trump, who 
continues to wield veto power on any such proposals.   
Asset class strategy 
Equities: Remain invested and continue to diversify.  
While investors initially overreacted, President Trump’s 
relatively conciliatory response has provided some welcome 
relief, helping markets recover strongly from their pre-market 
lows. Hence, we recommend investors avoid 
overreacting, remain invested and continue to diversify 
their equity exposure across sectors and regions.  
A few themes stand out from the early pre-market reaction to 
the mid-term elections. On the positive side, defensive stocks 
continue to fare well, while themes favoured by Democrats 
are also outperforming. Clean energy is a notable 
beneficiary, with solar stocks among the biggest winners

Policy uncertainty and a change in the innovation cycle 
is consistent with our view of a mid-cycle transition, 
where equity returns moderate but remain positive

recommend staying invested but diversification remains key

strong economy. High yield corporate spreads have widened

with BBs and Bs credit spreads staying relatively resilient, 
while CCCs have widened to distressed levels. For income-
focused investors, we would recommend staying within the 
crossover range of between BBB-BB.

### Page 1

The bank

economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.42 / 16 November 2026ECONEWS

ADVANCED ECONOMIES
EUROZONE
Robust growth but jobs stall and trade deficit rises. Q3 GDP growth
was confirmed at a robust 0.4% q/q, but employment was flat for the
first time since the Covid crisis. In September, intra-EU exports jum -
ped 1.7% m/m and 8.8% y/y. Stronger import growth widened the EU’s

rolling 12-month trade deficit with the rest of the world to EUR63.3bn
(0.4% of GDP). These are both indications of success of the EU’s struc -
tural efforts to boost its single market and have a more domestic de -
mand-driven growth. That said, the bilateral deficit with China reached
a new high of EUR38.9bn.
A decision on ECB leadership succession could be reached as early
as next week when EU Leaders meet on 18 November with a pac -
kage deal including Pablo Hernandez de Cos as President, a yet-to-be

named German chief economist, and former French cabinet member
Laurence Boone (currently running Santander France CIB) in charge of
market operations.
Upcoming: September industrial production (Monday); deadline for
nominations to ECB Board vacancies (which should provide clarity on
the full package including the President) and ECB Financial Stability
Review (Wednesday); Q3 negotiated wages (Friday).
France
A string of modest improvements. Unemployment edged down 0.1pp

to 8.2% in Q3, with a broadly stable activity rate. The Banque de France
business survey pointed to “moderate” October growth, driven main -
ly by manufacturing and market services. This aligns with our now -
cast of 0.2% q/q in Q4 GDP growth. Business starts increased 12% y/y
(excluding micro-entrepreneurs), while corporate bankruptcies rose
4.4% y/y over the 12 months to September, with a stable economic
weight (for more context, see our latest dashboard Business creations

and bankruptcies in France). The government brought forward the mi-
nimum-wage increase to December 1 rst and set it at 1.6%. The Court
of Auditors has published a report outlining “quickly implementable”
measures that would yield savings of EUR 32.2bn (1% of GDP) in public
finances (EUR20.9bn in spending and EUR 11.3bn in revenues), inclu -
ding a freeze on social benefits and an increase in the intermediate VAT
rate. The OAT-bund spread fell below 150 bps for the first time since

mid-October, before rising back to 154 as global yields rebounded.
Upcoming: INSEE November business survey (Friday).
Germany
Contrasted export performance. September exports rose a stron -
ger-than-expected 2.0% m/m, but imports surged 4.2%, narrowing the
trade surplus to EUR15.6bn from EUR17.9bn. The export picture was
sharply divided geographically: shipments to the EU rose 3.5% m/m,
while those to the US, China and the UK declined. Final October infla -

tion data confirmed the slowdown from preliminary estimates. Whole-
sale prices increased by 0.4, down from 1.9% in September, and in line
with market estimates.
Upcoming: November GfK Consumer Confidence (Tuesday).
Italy
Industry rebounds and trade surplus swells. Industrial production beat
expectations by rising by 1.2% m/m in September, driven by energy and
capital goods, although overall output remained 0.1% lower than a year
earlier notably owing to intermediate goods (-1.4% y/y) and consumer

goods (-3.0% y/y). On a monthly basis, chemical output contracted the
most (-8.3% m/m). The trade surplus over January-September reached
EUR36.8bn, up 37.7% YTD, as exports grew 4.7% against 2.8% for im -
ports.
Upcoming: October final HICP (Tuesday).
UNITED KINGDOM
Growth accelerates to 0.7% q/q in Q3, up from 0.5% in Q2, led by a
0.9% expansion in services. Gross fixed-capital formation increased
1.7%, while household and government consumption together rose

0.6%. Business investment grew 0.4%, confounding expectations of a
contraction. Net exports contributed negatively, however, as imports
outpaced exports.
Upcoming: September unemployment and average earnings (Tues -
day); October inflation (Wednesday); UK-EU “Reset” Summit (Friday).
UNITED STATES
Hot inflation revives hawkish Fed bets. Headline and core CPI both sur-
prised to the upside in October, with headline prices rising 0.4% m/m
and core CPI posting a third consecutive 0.3% increase, a pace incons -

istent with a return to the 2% target in the near term. Producer-price
pressures remained even stronger, with headline and core PPI reaching
6.5% and 5.0% y/y respectively (below consensus for headline, in line
for core).
Fed Governor Waller said a 50bp hike “should be considered” if core
inflation came in hot, while Dallas Fed President Logan subsequent -
ly suggested the FOMC might need to move more rapidly this winter.
The two-year Treasury yield rose more than 30bp over the week to

4.85% on 12 November, while the 10Y had its worst week since Libe -
ration Day. Meanwhile, existing-home sales fell another 5.5% m/m as
higher mortgage rates weighed on housing demand.
Upcoming: October retail sales and industrial production (Tuesday);
FOMC minutes, October building permits and housing starts (Wednes-
day).
JAPAN
Stalling growth and December BoJ hike signaled. GDP growth came
in at 0.0% q/q (-0.1% AR) in Q3. This below-consensus print ended

a run of three quarters of solid growth (consensus: 0.1%, prior: 0.4%).
Household consumption fell again (-0.3% q/q) under the combined
effect of inflation and adverse weather. Net exports also weighed on
growth due to a marked rebound in imports (2.5% q/q, prior: -1.3%).
On the bright side, business investment bounced back strongly
(1.6% q/q) after contracting in both H1 quarters.
The October Monetary Policy Meeting summary suggests policyma -
kers no longer see a need to be “overly cautious” about rate hikes,

with underlying inflation described as having “already reached 2%”
and the need to move the policy rate towards neutral now clearer.
Price pressures remain significant: PPI inflation accelerated to 7.1%
y/y in October. September real household spending nevertheless fell
2.8% m/m despite solid wage momentum.
Upcoming: October CPI inflation (Friday).

### Page 2

6
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.42 / 16 November 2026
The bank
for a changing

EMERGING ECONOMIES
ASIA
China
Weak domestic inflation, weak domestic demand, booming external
trade. CPI inflation slowed to 0.7% y/y in October and core inflation
to 0.8%. By contrast, producer price inflation continued to rise (rea -
ching 5.7% vs. 5.0% in September, driven by energy and chips), sug -
gesting margin compression for domestic consumer-facing businesses.

In October, export growth remained exceptionally strong at 35.2% y/y in
USD terms, supported by global demand for AI-related and green-tech
products. Integrated-circuit exports surged 137.1% in value, largely re-
flecting price effects, while auto exports rose 37.6%, primarily because
of higher volumes. Imports increased 41.6%, and the trade surplus
reached USD105.3bn in October and USD1,265bn over the 12 mon -
ths to October (from 1,180bn in 2025). Industrial production growth

slowed to 4.8% y/y in October from 5.5% in September, although the
electronics sector still posted solid performance. Meanwhile, domes -
tic demand remained weak, with both consumption and investment
slowing. Retail sales increased by 0.4% y/y in nominal terms (from
1.1%) and contracted in real terms, while the fall in nominal fixed-as -
set investment continued. The loss of momentum in economic growth
was also visible in services production growth (4.6% y/y vs. 4.8%).

India
Inflation breaks through 6%. Headline CPI accelerated to 6.1% y/y
in October from 5.5% previously, moving above the RBI’s 6% ceiling for
the first time in two years. Core inflation increased to 4.5%. With the
policy rate already raised 25bp to 5.5% in October, another 25bp in -
crease is expected at the December meeting.
CENTRAL EUROPE
Mild Q3 GDP uptick and mixed inflation data. The region’s Q3 GDP
growth was slightly higher compared to the previous quarter (2.2% y/y;

2.0% in Q2), mainly driven by Poland (3.6% y/y) and to a lesser extent
by Hungary (1.5% y/y) and Czech Rep. (1.6% y/y). Romania saw a po -
sitive growth (0.3% y/y) albeit soft, after three consecutive quarters in
contraction. Meanwhile, inflation print was mixed with an increase in
Hungary (2.0% y/y), Romania (6.9% y/y) and Poland (3.5% y/y). Inflation
was softer in Czech rep. (2.4% y/y) and Slovakia (3.0% y/y). Only Hun -
gary and Poland’s inflation rates are within the central bank’s target

range. Separately, Romania’s policy rate was kept on hold at 6.5% as
expected.
LATIN AMERICA
Persistent inflation pressures. In October, inflation rose in Brazil
(4.6% y/y), Mexico (3.8% y/y), and Colombia (6.3% y/y), driven by soa -
ring food prices, exacerbated by El Niño’s impact on agricultural out -
put. In Brazil, food inflation reached 5.7% y/y, while Mexico’s non-core
food prices jumped to 13.2% y/y, and Colombia’s food prices hit 7.3% y/y,

the highest since 2023. Central banks are now expected to maintain
rates on hold for an extended period.
Brazil
Modest fiscal adjustment. On 12 November, the Senate approved the
main provisions of a fiscal adjustment package aiming for a prima -
ry surplus of 0.5% of GDP in 2027. However, the proposed measures
are modest and do not address the structural rigidity of mandatory
spending. President Lula faces a difficult balancing act of reassuring
markets and preserving social commitments.

Paraguay
Ueno Bank collapse triggers confidence crisis. Paraguay’s sixth-largest
bank, Ueno Bank, was placed into resolution on 2nd November after its
capital fell below regulatory minimums, amid concerns over improper
related-party lending. The bank served nearly 3 million customers, re-
presenting around 60% of the adult population. Its collapse triggered a
major political crisis, culminating in President Santiago Pena’s removal
from office. No systemic banking crisis is expected, as other banks re -

main solvent and liquid. However, fiscal pressures are mounting, while
political uncertainty poses downside risks to growth. On November
11th, Fitch placed Paraguay’s (BB+) rating on Negative Watch, citing
risks to depositor confidence and fiscal stability.
COMMODITIES
Oil and gas: Physical pressures ease, but geopolitical risk lingers.
Brent rose by 3.4% over the week to USD96/bbl (after having fallen
below USD90 for a short while this week on hopes Iran-Us talks were

gaining traction). Physical supply tensions continued to ease: the IEA
reported that global crude supply exceeded demand in October for the
first time since the outbreak of the war and it expects the market to re-
turn to surplus in Q4. US commercial crude inventories rose by 4.7mb,
while strategic reserves fell by 2.6mb. Nevertheless, diesel cracks have
remained almost stable over the week (0.2% in the US and 0.4% in
Europe) on the back of persisting limited exports from the Gulf and

damages on Russian refineries. European gas prices rose 8% to EUR89/
MWh amid continuing constraints on Gulf LNG exports, colder weather
and outages at Norwegian gas facilities.
TO WATCH
Trilateral Summit between presidents Putin, Trump and Xi on the side-
lines of APEC Summit (Thu).
y/y = year-on-year
m/m = month-on-month
bp = basis point
pp = percentage point
mb = million barrels
